[9/19/25, 8:55:51 AM] bozo: guys how do we implement: This node should plot a single curve as well as its control points and a tangent line at at least one point
on the curve. We provide a starter implementation for a PlotTangentLine function. Currently, this
function just plots a single fixed line on the canvas. You will want to change it to plot a tangent line for
the particular curve instance. The code shows how you might go about rendering a line on the screen.
In particular, note that positions is a vector of points, and indices indexes into to positions—if
indices is [0, 1], this corresponds to a line segment between the first two points.
In order to display the curve, you will need to approximate it by subdividing it into line segments.
The constant N SUBDIV corresponds to the number of line segments that you should use to render a
single curve. Similar to how you display the tangent line, your indices can correspond to a polyline,
e.g., [0, 1, 1, 2, 2, 3, 3, 4]

std::vector<glm::vec3> new_nrm(bind_normals_.size());

for (size_t i = 0; i < NV; ++i) {
    const glm::vec4 p0(bind_positions_[i], 1.f);
    glm::vec4 p_acc(0.f);

    const glm::vec3 n0 = (i < bind_normals_.size() ? bind_normals_[i] : glm::vec3(0, 1, 0));
    glm::vec3 n_acc(0.f);

    const auto& weights = vertex_weights_[i];
    for (const auto& jw : weights) {
        const int   j = jw.first;
        const float w = jw.second;
        if (w <= 0.f) continue;
        if (j < 0 || static_cast<size_t>(j) >= J) continue;

        const glm::mat4 M = T[j] * bind_world_inv_[j];
        p_acc += w * (M * p0);

        const glm::mat3 A(M);
        n_acc += w * (A * n0);
    }

    new_pos[i] = (p_acc.w != 0.f) ? glm::vec3(p_acc) / p_acc.w : glm::vec3(p_acc);
    if (i < new_nrm.size()) {
        const float L = glm::length(n_acc);
        new_nrm[i] = (L > 1e-8f) ? (n_acc / L) : glm::vec3(0, 1, 0);
    }
}

SkeletonNode::SkeletonNode(const std::string& filename)
    : SceneNode(), draw_mode_(DrawMode::Skeleton) {

  // Make vertex objects

  cylinder_mesh_ = PrimitiveFactory::CreateCylinder(0.01f, 1.0f, 24);
  shader_ = std::make_shared<PhongShader>();

  bind_world_.resize(joint_node_ptrs.size());
  bind_world_inv_.resize(joint_node_ptrs.size());

      glm::mat4 Bj = joint_node_ptrs[j]->GetTransform().GetLocalToWorldMatrix();
      bind_world_[j] = Bj;
      bind_world_inv_[j] = glm::inverse(Bj);
  }

  // Force initial update.
  std::cerr << "test3";
  OnJointChanged();
  std::cerr << "test4";

[12/3/25, 11:28:20 AM] Danielle Knutson: Short version:
All those Deprecation warnings are harmless. The real build failure is just:

CMake Error … By not providing “FindSDL2.cmake” … Could not find … SDL2Config.cmake / sdl2-config.cmake

So:

What’s actually causing the error?

Your friend’s CMakeLists has a line like:

find_package(SDL2 REQUIRED)

CMake is trying to locate the SDL2 development package using either:
	•	a FindSDL2.cmake module in CMAKE_MODULE_PATH, or
	•	an installed SDL2 package that provides SDL2Config.cmake / sdl2-config.cmake.

On their Windows/VS setup, SDL2 isn’t installed (or isn’t visible to CMake), so find_package(SDL2) fails and configuration aborts.

Meanwhile:
	•	The GLFW bit is fine: No installed GLFW found … Include in build. just means it’ll build GLFW from the external folder.
	•	The cmake_minimum_required deprecation stuff is just CMake nagging about old versions; it’s not causing the error.

How to fix it (at a high level)

They need SDL2 development libraries installed and visible to CMake. Any of these patterns works:
	1.	Install SDL2 dev + point CMake at it
	•	Install SDL2 (dev) for Windows (via installer, vcpkg, MSYS2, or a prebuilt zip).
	•	Re-run CMake with something like:

-DSDL2_DIR="C:/path/to/SDL2/cmake"

where that directory contains SDL2Config.cmake.

	2.	Or add to CMAKE_PREFIX_PATH
So CMake can find SDL2Config.cmake automatically:

-DCMAKE_PREFIX_PATH="C:/path/to/SDL2/;..."


	3.	Or provide a FindSDL2.cmake in your repo
Drop a module called FindSDL2.cmake in a cmake/ folder and do:

set(CMAKE_MODULE_PATH "${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR}/cmake" ${CMAKE_MODULE_PATH})
find_package(SDL2 REQUIRED)



But bottom line:

The build dies because SDL2 isn’t installed / hooked up, not because of Windows SDK, Visual Studio, or the deprecation warnings.

[12/4/25, 12:25:59 PM] Danielle Knutson: def convolve3(im, kernel):
  from scipy import ndimage
  kernel_rows = list(kernel)
  kernel_clean = [list(row) for row in kernel_rows]
  kernel = np.array(kernel_clean, dtype=np.float64)

  center = (0, 0)
  r = ndimage.filters.convolve(im[:, :, 0], kernel, mode='reflect', origin=center)
  g = ndimage.filters.convolve(im[:, :, 1], kernel, mode='reflect', origin=center)
  b = ndimage.filters.convolve(im[:, :, 2], kernel, mode='reflect', origin=center)
  return np.dstack([r, g, b])

def horiGaussKernel(sigma, truncate=3):
    """Return 1D normalized Gaussian kernel."""
    radius = int(sigma * truncate)
    x = np.arange(-radius, radius + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    k = np.exp(-0.5 * (x / sigma) ** 2)
    k /= k.sum()
    return k

def gauss2D(sigma=2, truncate=3):
    """Return 2D normalized Gaussian kernel as outer product of 1D kernel."""
    k1d = horiGaussKernel(sigma, truncate)
    kerker = np.outer(k1d, k1d)
    kerker /= kerker.sum()
    return kerker

[2/18/26, 12:05:52 AM] bozo: This is my write up for 6: 
same hour), the assignment X_s,h = (t, c) and X_s',h = (t', c') is only valid if t != t' (in other words the tutors are different) OR X_s,h = (None, None) OR X_s',h = (None, None)


Goal: For this CSP formulation an assignment for all X_s,h is valid if all students get help for all their classes




Problem Definition

Let the set of tutors be T, the set of students be S, the set of hours in the week be H and the set of classes be C
Each tutor t ∈ T is qualified to teach a subset of classes C_t ⊆ C and is only available during a subset of hours H_t ⊆ H. Each student s requires help in a subset of classes C_s ⊆ C and is able to recieve tutoring their available hours H_s ⊆ H
Variables

For this problem I define a set of variables X_s,h for each s and h to account for what happens to student s at hour h. As a result, let the corresponding domains for these variables be D_s,h = {(None, None), (t, c) for all t and c in T and C}. What I mean by that is that for any X_s,h, student s gets either no tutoring (hence the None) or gets tutored by tutor t for class c for that hour h.

Contraints

Every X_s,h is subject to the following unary and binary constraints.

Unary Constraint: If X_s,h = (t, c) then c has to be in C_s AND C_t, and h has to be in both H_s AND H_t for that s and t.
Binary Constraint: Since at any hour h, a tutor can teach at most one student (and vice versa) for at most 1 class, then a constraint can be defined such that for every hour and every distinct pair of students s and s' (in the same hour), the assignment X_s,h = (t, c) and X_s',h = (t', c') is only valid if t != t' (in other words the tutors are different) OR X_s,h = (None, None) OR X_s',h = (None, None)
Goal: For this CSP formulation an assignment for all X_s,h is valid if all students get help for all their classes
